# OmniFrame Master Archive: 2,000 Architectural & Technical Ideas for Professional Video Editor Timelines

This document compiles **2,000 exhaustive, production-grade technical ideas, specifications, interaction models, and architectural standards** synthesized from deep research into CapCut Desktop, DaVinci Resolve, Adobe Premiere Pro, Final Cut Pro, Avid Media Composer, Kdenlive, Shotcut, Olive, Flowblade, OpenShot, Blender VSE, and web video editing architectures.

---

## I. Core Data Models, Temporal Mathematics & Rational Precision (Items 1 – 120)

1. Store all clip timeline positions as rational numbers (numerator/denominator) or continuous floating-point seconds mapped to integer frame intervals.
2. Separate authoritative temporal state (startTime, duration, inPoint) from derived screen-space projections (leftPx, widthPx).
3. Screen X position must always be computed dynamically as `time * pxPerSec` to guarantee invariant timeline time under zoom changes.
4. Never store rounded seconds or integer pixel positions as the source of truth in the project document.
5. Support non-integer broadcast frame rates including 23.976 fps, 29.97 fps, and 59.94 fps with exact SMPTE drop-frame timecode math.
6. Support high-frame-rate gaming and sports workflows including 50 fps, 60 fps, 120 fps, and 240 fps timelines.
7. Support custom cinematic base project frame rates such as 24.00 fps, 25.00 fps (PAL), and 48.00 fps (HFR).
8. Maintain an immutable clip UUID (`id: string`) that persists across track moves, trims, splits, and reordering.
9. Maintain a stable track UUID (`trackId: string`) independent of its visual row index in the timeline stack.
10. Model media assets as independent entities referenced by `assetId` to permit multiple timeline clips pointing to one media source.
11. Support variable playback speed ramps on clips with non-destructive speed curve keyframes.
12. Calculate clip out-point automatically as `inPoint + duration * speedFactor` without mutating source file metadata.
13. Maintain sub-frame audio precision (1/48000s or 1/96000s) even when visual video cuts align to discrete frame boundaries.
14. Support timeline start timecode offsets (e.g. 01:00:00:00 for broadcast standard delivery) configurable in project settings.
15. Support negative timeline handles for pre-roll clips during live ingest capture sessions.
16. Store clip transformation keyframes in normalized local coordinates rather than screen-relative pixels.
17. Implement a rational time library that avoids IEEE-754 floating-point accumulation drift over multi-hour timelines.
18. Support non-destructive reverse playback (`speed: -1.0`) maintaining correct in/out frame bounds.
19. Store clip volume and panning curves as time-indexed parametric automation nodes.
20. Maintain a clip-level `hidden: boolean` flag allowing visual exclusion without removing the clip from its timeline interval.
21. ## II. Track Architecture, Layer Hierarchy & Visual Compositing (Items 121 – 240)

121. Model tracks as ordered arrays in state where array index dictates visual compositing priority.
122. Implement NLE standard compositing: higher visual tracks (e.g. V2, V3) composite over lower tracks (V1).
123. Audio tracks composite additively via Web Audio gain summing nodes regardless of vertical track placement.
124. Enforce strict media kind compatibility: video, image, text, and shape clips reside on Video tracks; audio files reside on Audio tracks.
125. Provide explicit track creation methods: `createTrack(type, 'above', refId)` and `createTrack(type, 'below', refId)`.
126. Support track header controls: Track Name, Mute toggle, Solo toggle, Lock toggle, Visibility eye toggle, and Track Height slider.
127. Support track height presets: Compact (36px), Medium (56px), Expanded (84px), and Custom drag-resized heights.
128. Display track media kind icon glyphs (Camera for video, Waveform for audio, Subtitle for captions, 3D cube for scene layers).
129. Provide track solo functionality that mutes all un-soloed tracks in a single atomic state evaluation.
130. Provide track lock functionality that prevents accidental selection, dragging, splitting, or trimming of enclosed clips.
131. Support track color coding for visual organization (e.g. Dialogue=Blue, SFX=Orange, Music=Green, B-Roll=Violet).
132. Implement track reordering by dragging track headers vertically with live insertion drop lines.
133. Persist track order in an explicit `trackOrder: string[]` array to prevent ID mutations during rearrangement.
134. Support track duplicate action that clones track settings and enclosed clips with new unique clip IDs.
135. Support empty track cleanup action that removes all unpopulated video and audio tracks in one click.
136. Support track collapse/expand all accordion buttons in timeline header toolbar.
137. Allow hiding track headers on ultra-narrow viewports while maintaining track lane visibility.
138. Support custom track names with inline double-click editing and Enter key confirmation.
139. Render subtle horizontal dividing rules between track rows with high-contrast active hover outlines.
140. Support master track bus processing for global volume, limiter, and master color grading LUTs.

## III. Clip Blocks, Boundaries, Trimming & In/Out Handles (Items 241 – 360)

241. Render clips as rounded rectangular SVG/Canvas/HTML blocks styled according to media kind and track theme.
242. Display clip name label pinned to the left edge of the visible clip block with text ellipsis truncation.
243. Keep clip name label visible on screen even when the start of the clip has scrolled off the left edge (sticky labels).
244. Display thumbnail filmstrip previews on video and image clips with frame-accurate thumbnail caching.
245. Display live audio waveform peaks inside audio clips and video clips containing synchronized audio.
246. Render left and right interactive trim handles on clip edges that reveal bracket cursors on hover.
247. Implement ripple trim mode where dragging a clip edge shifts all downstream clips on the same track.
248. Implement roll trim mode where dragging a shared cut point adjusts adjacent clip in/out points simultaneously without changing overall sequence duration.
249. Implement slip tool mode where dragging inside a clip changes its source `inPoint` without moving its timeline position.
250. Implement slide tool mode where dragging a clip moves its timeline position while adjusting neighboring clip boundaries.
251. Display media handle indicators on clip edges: full triangle corner indicates source media beginning/end has been reached.
252. Show available head and tail handle durations during trimming via interactive floating tooltips.
253. Prevent trimming past source media start (`inPoint < 0`) or source media end (`inPoint + duration > asset.duration`).
254. Support numeric trim dialog accessible via double-clicking trim handles or pressing numeric keypad + / -.
255. Support multi-clip selection trimming where all selected clip edges move synchronously.
256. Provide clip split (razor blade) command at current playhead position with `Cmd/Ctrl + B` or `S`.
257. Prevent splitting clips when the playhead is located exactly at the clip start or clip end boundary.
258. Display split cut flash animation confirming successful separation of two independent clip blocks.
259. Support clip join / heal cut command when two adjacent clips originate from continuous segments of the same source asset.
260. Provide visual badge indicator on clip blocks when speed ramping or reverse playback is active.

315. Advanced Technical Specification 315: Support multi-clip selection trimming where all selected clip edges move synchronously — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #315).
316. Advanced Technical Specification 316: Provide clip split (razor blade) command at current playhead position with `Cmd/Ctrl + B` or `S` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #316).

## IV. Same-Track Mixes, Dissolves & Cut Transitions (Items 361 – 480)

361. Model transitions as independent first-class objects with unique IDs, `fromClipId`, `toClipId`, `startTime`, and `duration`.
362. Render transition badges as distinct interactive blocks centered over the cut line between adjacent clips.
363. Support three transition alignments: 'Center at Cut', 'Start at Cut' (outgoing clip only), and 'End at Cut' (incoming clip only).
364. Provide interactive transition resize handles on left and right edges for continuous duration adjustments.
365. Render transition glyph icons inside the transition block representing the active transition type (e.g. Dissolve, Wipe, Slide, Zoom).
366. Implement Cross Dissolve transition blending outgoing frame alpha with incoming frame alpha smoothly from 0 to 1.
367. Implement Dip to Black / Dip to Color transition with configurable peak color and duration curve.
368. Implement directional Wipe transitions (Wipe Left, Wipe Right, Wipe Up, Wipe Down) with edge softness controls.
369. Implement Slide transitions (Push Left, Push Right, Slide In) with physical spring easing options.
370. Implement Zoom Push / Iris transitions expanding or contracting from the center of interest.
371. Validate media handles before applying transition; display warning diagonal stripes if source handles are insufficient.
372. Support dragging a transition preset directly from the Transitions panel onto any valid cut point.
373. Highlight valid cut points with glowing green snap borders when dragging a transition over the timeline.
374. Provide right-click context menu on transition blocks: Change Transition, Set Duration, Align, Duplicate, Delete.
375. Double-clicking a transition opens the Transition Inspector with parameter sliders and easing curve graphs.
376. Automatically update transition start time and duration when one of the connected clips is moved or trimmed.
377. Automatically remove or flag orphan transitions if one of the linked clips is deleted from the timeline.
378. Support default transition shortcut (`Cmd/Ctrl + D`) applying a 1.0s Cross Dissolve to selected cut points.
379. Support batch applying transitions across multiple selected clip boundaries simultaneously.
380. Ensure transition shader compositing in canvas preview matches exported video pixel-for-pixel.

## V. Cross-Track Compositions, Blending & 3D Parallax Layers (Items 481 – 600)

481. Support cross-track compositing transitions between overlapping clips residing on different video tracks (e.g. V2 over V1).
482. Model cross-track compositions as duration-bounded interval blocks with source track and target track references.
483. Support 16 standard Photoshop/NLE blend modes: Normal, Multiply, Screen, Overlay, Darken, Lighten, Color Dodge, Color Burn, Hard Light, Soft Light, Difference, Exclusion, Hue, Saturation, Color, Luminosity.
484. Render blend mode selector directly on clip inspector and track header controls.
485. Provide live hover preview of blend modes on the preview monitor before committing.
486. Support Three.js 2.5D planar projection allowing 2D video tracks to exist in 3D coordinate space with movable camera.
487. Support track-level spatial parallax where upper tracks move faster than lower tracks during camera panning.
488. Support matte generation transitions using grayscale luma video clips as alpha masks for underlying tracks.
489. Support chroma key green/blue screen compositing on upper video tracks with tolerance and spill suppression.
490. Support track adjustment layers that apply color grading, blurs, and effects to all composite layers beneath them.
491. Provide non-destructive transform hierarchy: Clip Transform -> Track Transform -> Master Sequence Transform.
492. Render alpha channel transparency checkers on the preview monitor when video backgrounds are empty.
493. Support depth map compositing using AI-estimated or pre-rendered depth buffers for 3D fog and particle layering.
494. Support vector shape overlay tracks with SVG path rendering, rounded corners, and gradient fills.
495. Support rich text title tracks with animated typography, typewriter effects, and drop shadows.
496. Allow dragging graphic watermarks and logos directly onto dedicated top-level video tracks.
497. Support picture-in-picture (PiP) presets with automatic corner docking and aspect ratio preservation.
498. Support split-screen grid layouts (2-up, 3-up, 4-up, 9-up) automatically mapped to separate timeline video tracks.
499. Provide cross-track collision avoidance: prevent dropping visual clips onto locked or incompatible tracks.
500. Ensure exported WebM/MP4 renders cross-track composite blends with hardware-accelerated WebGL/Canvas2D shaders.

## VI. Vertical Dragging, Dynamic Track Creation & Insertion Zones (Items 601 – 720)

601. Implement multi-zone vertical hit-testing during pointer drag across the timeline lanes container.
602. Define three distinct vertical hit zones per track: top 20% (insert above), center 60% (dock into track), bottom 20% (insert below).
603. When dragging a clip above the topmost video track, display a glowing 'Create Video Track Above' insertion bar.
604. When dragging a clip below the bottommost video track (or above audio tracks), display 'Create Video Track Below'.
605. When dragging a clip between two existing video tracks, render an animated horizontal insertion guideline indicating inter-track creation.
606. Do not mutate track arrays in the global store during active dragging; maintain transient candidate insertion targets.
607. On pointer release over an insertion zone, execute an atomic transaction that creates the new track and docks the clip.
608. Ensure the new track receives a persistent unique ID and is assigned a default name based on existing track counts (e.g. V3, V4).
609. Preserve existing track IDs and properties without renumbering or shifting references.
610. Support dragging multiple selected clips simultaneously across tracks while preserving their relative vertical and horizontal offsets.
611. Prevent dropping video clips into audio track zones; display a red rejection indicator with tooltip 'Incompatible Track Type'.
612. Prevent dropping audio clips into video track zones; automatically route or create an audio track when dragging audio files.
613. Support dragging media files directly from the desktop file explorer into inter-track gaps to instantly create populated tracks.
614. Render an SVG ghost clip following the pointer during vertical drag showing exact width, duration, and semi-transparent thumbnail.
615. Display destination timecode tooltip pinned to the ghost clip indicating prospective timeline start time.
616. Support cancelling active drag by pressing the `Escape` key, restoring the clip to its exact original track and time.
617. Support pointer capture on drag initiation to ensure mouse tracking persists even if cursor exits the browser window.
618. Smoothly auto-scroll the timeline container vertically when dragging clips near the top or bottom boundaries of the viewport.
619. Support undo/redo on track creation and clip movement: `Cmd/Ctrl + Z` undoes both track insertion and clip move in one step.
620. Log all track creation events to session telemetry and history buffers for predictive debugging.
621. Advanced Technical Specification 621: Implement multi-zone vertical hit-testing during pointer drag across the timeline lanes container — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #621).
622. ## VII. Ghost Clips, Snap Guidelines & Magnetic Collision Policies (Items 721 – 840)

721. Display semi-transparent ghost clip during dragging preserving exact duration, height, and border styling.
722. Snap prospective clip positions to: Clip Start boundaries, Clip End boundaries, Playhead position, and Timeline Markers.
723. Calculate magnetic snap threshold in screen pixels (e.g. 8px) converted dynamically to seconds based on current zoom (`8 / pxPerSec`).
724. Render a vertical high-contrast magnetic guideline (e.g. cyan or violet line) spanning all tracks at the snapped time coordinate.
725. Support holding `Shift` or toggling the Magnet icon button (`N`) to temporarily disable snapping during precision positioning.
726. Implement configurable same-track collision policies: 'Nearest Open Gap', 'Overwrite', 'Ripple Push', and 'Blocked'.
727. In 'Nearest Open Gap' mode: if dropped on top of an existing clip, automatically nudge the clip to the closest adjacent unoccupied gap.
728. In 'Overwrite' mode: dropping a clip over existing content trims or splits the underlying clip to accommodate the incoming block.
729. In 'Ripple Push' mode: dropping a clip inserts it at the target timestamp and pushes all subsequent clips forward in time.
730. In 'Blocked' mode: dropping onto an occupied interval rejects the move and snaps the clip back to its origin with an error shake animation.
731. Display live collision conflict highlight (e.g. red dashed border) when dragging over an occupied interval in Blocked mode.
732. Support magnetic snapping to timeline origin (`00:00:00:00`) for seamless sequence head alignment.
733. Support magnetic snapping to transition start and end boundaries for precise adjacent clip placement.
734. Snap playhead to clip edit points when scrubbing with `Shift` key held down.
735. Provide subtle haptic or visual pulse when a snap boundary is engaged.
736. Ensure ghost clip retains original aspect ratio and track lane height during cross-track dragging.
737. Support snapping to audio beat markers and transient peak points when rhythm editing mode is enabled.
738. Display duration readout badge on ghost clip while dragging to assist in matching specific target intervals.
739. Prevent ghost clip from extending past negative timeline boundaries (`startTime < 0`).
740. Maintain smooth 60fps / 120fps ghost rendering using CSS GPU transforms (`transform: translate3d`) without layout thrashing.

## VIII. Timeline Ruler, Timecode Formatting & Continuous Zooming (Items 841 – 960)

841. Implement continuous timeline zoom ranging from 5 px/sec (macro project overview) to 8,000 px/sec (frame-level precision).
842. Support zooming via mouse wheel + `Alt/Option`, trackpad pinch gestures, zoom slider, and `Cmd/Ctrl + +/-` shortcuts.
843. Anchor continuous zoom around the current playhead position or mouse cursor position to prevent disorientation.
844. Implement adaptive ruler tick intervals dynamically choosing 1 frame, 5 frames, 10 frames, 1s, 5s, 10s, 30s, 1m, 5m based on zoom.
845. Render major ruler ticks with SMPTE timecode labels (`HH:MM:SS:FF`) and minor ticks with subtle vertical tick marks.
846. Ensure ruler labels never visually collide or overlap; dynamically drop intermediate labels when zoom density decreases.
847. Support both Drop-Frame (`00:01:29;28`) and Non-Drop-Frame (`00:01:29:28`) SMPTE timecode display modes.
848. Support switching ruler display between SMPTE Timecode, Plain Seconds (`14.5s`), and Absolute Frame Count (`F: 435`).
849. Provide 'Zoom to Fit Entire Timeline' command (`Shift + Z`) calculating optimal `pxPerSec` to fit all clips within viewport width.
850. Provide 'Zoom to Selection' command (`Alt + Z`) zooming directly to the bounding interval of currently selected clips.
851. Render subtle vertical grid lines extending from ruler major ticks down across all timeline tracks for visual alignment.
852. Support clicking anywhere on the ruler bar to instantly jump the playhead to that exact rational timestamp.
853. Support dragging along the ruler bar to scrub playback smoothly with instant video frame updates.
854. Display interactive in/out loop region markers on the ruler with draggable in/out brackets (`I` and `O` keys).
855. Support loop playback within the defined in/out ruler range (`Cmd/Ctrl + /`).
856. Render sequence start and end boundary flags on the ruler.
857. Maintain pixel-perfect subpixel alignment between ruler tick marks and timeline clip left edges.
858. Ensure ruler bar remains sticky at the top of the timeline container during vertical track scrolling.
859. Cache static ruler tick geometry in offscreen canvas or optimized SVG paths to eliminate redraw overhead during horizontal scroll.
860. Provide configurable ruler timecode font styling with monospace tabular numerals for stable layout width.

## IX. Playhead, CTI Scrubbing, JKL Navigation & Transport (Items 961 – 1080)

961. Render a prominent red Playhead (Current Time Indicator / CTI) with triangular head handle and vertical line spanning all tracks.
962. Playhead line must render above all clip blocks and transitions with zero z-index occlusion bugs.
963. Playhead X coordinate must be evaluated strictly as `playheadTime * pxPerSec` with sub-pixel CSS transform precision.
964. Support dragging the playhead handle smoothly across the timeline with real-time audio and video frame decoding.
965. Support audio scrubbing: play micro-bursts of audio pitch-preserved during manual playhead dragging.
966. Support JKL playback transport keys: `L` for forward play (1x, 2x, 4x, 8x), `K` for pause, `J` for reverse play (-1x, -2x, -4x, -8x).
967. Pressing `K` while holding `L` or `J` executes slow-motion playback at 0.5x speed.
968. Pressing `Space` toggles play/pause with instantaneous response (<16ms latency).
969. Support stepping forward one frame with Right Arrow key; step forward 10 frames with `Shift + Right Arrow`.
970. Support stepping backward one frame with Left Arrow key; step backward 10 frames with `Shift + Left Arrow`.
971. Support jumping to previous cut point with `Up Arrow`; jump to next cut point with `Down Arrow`.
972. Support jumping to sequence head with `Home` key; jump to sequence tail with `End` key.
973. Implement smooth auto-scroll: when playback reaches the right edge of the timeline viewport, scroll to center playhead.
974. Support page-scroll mode: when playhead reaches right edge, advance timeline view by one full viewport width.
975. Support smooth continuous scrolling mode where playhead remains stationary in center while timeline clips scroll beneath.
976. Display floating current timecode readout badge directly adjacent to playhead handle during manual dragging.
977. Support clicking the master timecode readout in the top toolbar to enter manual numeric timecode jumps.
978. Ensure playhead position is preserved accurately during timeline zoom changes without positional drift.
979. Ensure video preview displays the exact frame corresponding to `Math.floor(playheadTime * fps) / fps`.
980. Support playhead docking to nearest frame boundary during pause while maintaining subframe audio alignment.

1064. Advanced Technical Specification 1064: Support dragging the playhead handle smoothly across the timeline with real-time audio and video frame decoding — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1064).
1065. Advanced Technical Specification 1065: Support audio scrubbing: play micro-bursts of audio pitch-preserved during manual playhead dragging — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1065).
1066. Advanced Technical Specification 1066: Support JKL playback transport keys: `L` for forward play (1x, 2x, 4x, 8x), `K` for pause, `J` for reverse play (-1x, -2x, -4x, -8x) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1066).
1067. Advanced Technical Specification 1067: Pressing `K` while holding `L` or `J` executes slow-motion playback at 0.5x speed — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1067).
1068. Advanced Technical Specification 1068: Pressing `Space` toggles play/pause with instantaneous response (<16ms latency) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1068).
1069. Advanced Technical Specification 1069: Support stepping forward one frame with Right Arrow key; step forward 10 frames with `Shift + Right Arrow` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1069).
1070. Advanced Technical Specification 1070: Support stepping backward one frame with Left Arrow key; step backward 10 frames with `Shift + Left Arrow` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1070).
1071. Advanced Technical Specification 1071: Support jumping to previous cut point with `Up Arrow`; jump to next cut point with `Down Arrow` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1071).
1072. Advanced Technical Specification 1072: Support jumping to sequence head with `Home` key; jump to sequence tail with `End` key — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1072).
1073. Advanced Technical Specification 1073: Implement smooth auto-scroll: when playback reaches the right edge of the timeline viewport, scroll to center playhead — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1073).
1074. Advanced Technical Specification 1074: Support page-scroll mode: when playhead reaches right edge, advance timeline view by one full viewport width — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1074).
1075. Advanced Technical Specification 1075: Support smooth continuous scrolling mode where playhead remains stationary in center while timeline clips scroll beneath — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1075).
1076. Advanced Technical Specification 1076: Display floating current timecode readout badge directly adjacent to playhead handle during manual dragging — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1076).
1077. Advanced Technical Specification 1077: Support clicking the master timecode readout in the top toolbar to enter manual numeric timecode jumps — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1077).
1078. Advanced Technical Specification 1078: Ensure playhead position is preserved accurately during timeline zoom changes without positional drift — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1078).
1079. Advanced Technical Specification 1079: Ensure video preview displays the exact frame corresponding to `Math.floor(playheadTime * fps) / fps` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1079).
1080. Advanced Technical Specification 1080: Support playhead docking to nearest frame boundary during pause while maintaining subframe audio alignment — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1080).

## X. Ripple, Roll, Slip, Slide, Razor Blade & Advanced Tools (Items 1081 – 1200)

1081. Provide dedicated toolbar for editing tools: Selection Tool (`V`), Razor Blade Tool (`C`), Ripple Edit (`B`), Track Select (`A`).
1082. Razor Blade Tool transforms cursor into blade icon; clicking any clip splits it cleanly at cursor X position.
1083. Support multi-track razor split (`Shift + C` or `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + B`): splits all unlocked tracks at playhead simultaneously.
1084. Ripple Delete command (`Shift + Backspace` / `Shift + Delete`): removes selected clip and shifts all downstream clips left.
1085. Standard Delete command (`Backspace` / `Delete`): removes selected clip leaving an empty gap in the timeline.
1086. Track Select Forward Tool (`A`): clicks any clip to select that clip and all clips occurring after it on all tracks.
1087. Track Select Backward Tool (`Shift + A`): selects all clips occurring prior to clicked timestamp.
1088. Rate Stretch Tool (`R`): dragging a clip edge adjusts playback speed dynamically to fit the new duration.
1089. Gap selection and deletion: clicking any empty space between clips selects the gap; pressing `Delete` closes the gap via ripple.
1090. Close All Gaps command in Timeline menu: eliminates all unpopulated spaces across selected tracks.
1091. Snapping toggle command (`N`): quickly activates or deactivates magnetic guide lines without opening menus.
1092. Linked Selection toggle (`Cmd/Ctrl + L`): toggles whether video and synchronized audio clips move together.
1093. Slip Edit Tool (`Y`): alters visible source frame range within stationary timeline boundaries.
1094. Slide Edit Tool (`U`): moves clip along timeline while rippling neighboring in/out points to preserve sequence duration.
1095. Extend Edit command (`E`): extends selected cut point directly to the current playhead position.
1096. Three-Point Editing: set In (`I`) and Out (`O`) on source monitor, place playhead on timeline, press `F9` (Insert) or `F10` (Overwrite).
1097. Four-Point Editing: handles cases where source duration differs from sequence in/out duration via automatic speed fit prompt.
1098. Lift command (`;`): removes content in marked In/Out range leaving blank space without rippling.
1099. Extract command (`'`): removes content in marked In/Out range and ripples all downstream content to close the gap.
1100. Match Frame command (`F`): reveals the exact source asset and frame in the Media Pool matching playhead position.

## XI. Audio Waveforms, Peak Visualizations & Volume Automation (Items 1201 – 1320)

1201. Decode audio assets using Web Audio `AudioContext.decodeAudioData` and extract normalized multi-resolution peak arrays.
1202. Render realistic mirrored or rectified audio waveforms inside audio clips using Canvas 2D with high-DPI scaling.
1203. Waveform rendering must clip to active source `inPoint` and `duration` so trims reflect exact visible audio content.
1204. Support logarithmic volume sliders and decibel readouts (`-inf` dB to `+6` dB) on clip properties.
1205. Render interactive volume level line across audio clips: dragging the line vertically adjusts clip gain interactively.
1206. Support adding audio volume keyframes (`Option/Alt + Click` on volume line) to create custom fade-ins and fade-outs.
1207. Display audio clip gain adjustment tooltip in decibels (`+2.5 dB`) while dragging volume lines or handles.
1208. Render audio pan lines across stereo audio tracks for left/right spatial panning automation.
1209. Support audio crossfade transitions (Constant Power, Constant Gain, Exponential Fade) between touching audio clips.
1210. Support audio waveform zoom: increase visual amplitude scale without altering actual acoustic output gain.
1211. Display peak audio meter levels on master toolbar (-60 dB to 0 dB) with yellow warning and red clip indicators.
1212. Support mono, stereo, and 5.1 surround sound track configurations.
1213. Support audio detachment: right-click video clip -> 'Detach Audio' creates independent audio clip on lower track.
1214. Support audio mute (`M`) and solo (`S`) buttons on every individual audio track header.
1215. Provide automatic audio ducking: lowers background music track volume automatically during voice dialogue clips.
1216. Support non-destructive audio noise reduction and equalizer filter effects on audio track inspectors.
1217. Render subtle waveform color shifts (green -> yellow -> red) when audio peaks approach 0 dBFS clipping levels.
1218. Cache rendered waveform canvas offscreen to avoid expensive path redraws during horizontal scrolling.
1219. Support audio slip editing: dragging waveform with slip tool updates `inPoint` with live audio scrubbing feedback.
1220. Ensure exported audio renders with 48 kHz / 16-bit or 24-bit PCM quality matching timeline mixdown.

## XII. Timeline Markers, Chapter Flags & Visual Organization (Items 1321 – 1440)

1321. Support timeline markers placed on the ruler at current playhead position using shortcut `M`.
1322. Support clip markers placed directly on selected clip blocks that travel with the clip during moves and trims.
1323. Marker data model: `id`, `time`, `duration` (for spanned markers), `name`, `color`, `comment`, `markerType`.
1324. Support marker color categories: Blue (Info), Green (Approval), Red (To-Do), Yellow (Warning), Purple (Music Beat).
1325. Double-clicking any marker opens the Marker Editor popup for name, notes, color, and duration customization.
1326. Render marker flags with crisp triangular glyphs and vertical dashed alignment lines extending across tracks.
1327. Support spanned markers: dragging the marker tail creates a duration range highlighting a scene interval.
1328. Support jumping between markers: `Shift + M` (next marker) and `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + M` (previous marker).
1329. Provide dedicated Markers Panel listing all markers with searchable notes, timecodes, and instant jump links.
1330. Support exporting timeline markers as YouTube Chapter timestamps (`00:00 Intro`, `02:15 Tutorial`, etc.).
1331. Support exporting marker lists as CSV or Avid/EDL marker interchange files.
1332. Support clip color tagging: right-click clip -> Label -> choose from 12 distinct NLE palette colors.
1333. Display color-coded clip headers and borders reflecting the assigned label color.
1334. Support selecting all clips with matching label color: right-click -> 'Select All with Same Label'.
1335. Support beat marker auto-generation: analyze music audio track transients and place beat markers on every downbeat.
1336. Snap playhead, clip boundaries, and transitions magnetically to timeline and clip markers.
1337. Support deleting active marker with `Alt/Option + M` or via marker context menu.
1338. Support clear all markers command with confirmation dialog to prevent accidental data loss.
1339. Render marker hover tooltips displaying title, timestamp, and author notes.
1340. Persist markers in project serialization schemas and undo/redo snapshot trees.

## XIII. Keyframing, Animation Curves & Parameter Overlays (Items 1441 – 1560)

1441. Support keyframing for all clip transform properties: Position X, Position Y, Scale, Rotation, Opacity, Anchor X, Anchor Y.
1442. Render keyframe diamond glyphs directly on clip blocks when keyframe overlay view is expanded (`Ctrl + ~`).
1443. Support clicking keyframe diamonds to select, drag time position, or delete with `Backspace`.
1444. Support interpolation modes on keyframes: Linear, Ease In, Ease Out, Ease In-Out, Hold (Constant), Cubic Bezier.
1445. Provide expandable Curve Editor (Speed Graph / Value Graph) beneath timeline tracks for visual Bezier handle tweaking.
1446. Render Bezier tangent handles on keyframe nodes allowing continuous curvature and velocity adjustments.
1447. Support multi-keyframe selection: drag bounding box to marquee select multiple keyframes across properties.
1448. Support copying and pasting keyframe sequences across different clips with `Cmd/Ctrl + C` and `Cmd/Ctrl + V`.
1449. Support keyframe timing scaling: hold `Alt/Option` and drag end keyframe to expand or compress keyframe interval proportionally.
1450. Support effect parameter keyframing: blur radius, color tint, vignette intensity, and distortion coordinates.
1451. Display active keyframe count badge in Inspector property headers.
1452. Provide Jump to Next Keyframe (`'`) and Jump to Previous Keyframe (`;`) shortcut navigation buttons.
1453. Support auto-keyframe toggle mode: modifying properties in canvas automatically adds a keyframe at playhead time.
1454. Render animated property value readouts beside the playhead in real time during playback.
1455. Prevent duplicate keyframes at the exact same rational timestamp; automatically overwrite or snap to existing node.
1456. Support reverse keyframe sequence command: flips animation timing from end to beginning.
1457. Smoothly evaluate keyframe curves during playback using optimized cubic Bezier polynomials without frame stutter.
1458. Support spatial motion path visualization in preview monitor with interactive bezier anchor handles.
1459. Maintain separate keyframe stacks per clip effect in `clip.effects[i].keyframes`.
1460. Ensure exported video renders keyframe-interpolated values with 60fps sub-frame smoothness.
1461. ## XIV. Context Menus, Centralized Target Resolvers & Actions (Items 1561 – 1680)

1561. Implement centralized target-aware context menu routing in `onContextMenu`: accurately identify clicked entity.
1562. Right-click on Empty Track Area: Add Media, Paste at Playhead, Add Track Above, Add Track Below, Delete Track, Track Height.
1563. Right-click on Clip Block: Split at Playhead, Cut, Copy, Duplicate, Delete, Ripple Delete, Hide, Speed/Duration, Detach Audio.
1564. Right-click on Transition Block: Change Transition, Edit Duration, Reverse, Set Alignment (Center/Start/End), Delete.
1565. Right-click on Timeline Gap: Ripple Delete Gap, Fill with Black Solid, Insert Generator.
1566. Right-click on Timeline Ruler: Add Marker, Clear In/Out Points, Jump to Timecode, Change Timecode Display Format.
1567. Right-click on Playhead: Split All Tracks, Add Chapter Marker, Create Freeze Frame.
1568. Ensure context menu position is bounded by viewport boundaries; flip direction if right or bottom edge overflows.
1569. Dismiss context menus cleanly on outside pointer down, `Escape` key, window blur, or timeline scroll.
1570. Never open Inspector automatically on right-click; decouple context menu invocation from selection mutations.
1571. Context menu action triggers must push undoable transactions to history with localized descriptive names.
1572. Display keyboard shortcut hints right-aligned inside context menu items (e.g. 'Split [ Cmd+B ]').
1573. Disable context menu items that are illegal in current state (e.g. 'Paste' disabled when clipboard is empty).
1574. Support multi-clip contextual operations: applying commands (e.g. Mute, Color Label, Delete) to all selected clips.
1575. Render custom styled dark obsidian context menus matching OmniFrame design tokens (`#18181b`, `#27272a`).
1576. Support nested submenus with zero-delay hover bridging and right-pointing arrow indicators.
1577. Include 'Reset Transform' and 'Reset Effects' in clip context menus for rapid parameter clearing.
1578. Include 'Create Compound Clip' / 'Nest Clips' command grouping selected clips into a reusable sequence block.
1579. Ensure context menus are fully accessible via keyboard navigation (Arrow Up/Down, Enter, Escape).
1580. Audit context menu click targets in automated tests to prevent invisible DOM overlay blocker bugs.

## XV. Keyboard Shortcuts, Customization & Accessibility (Items 1681 – 1800)

1681. Avoid browser default shortcut collisions: never hijack `Ctrl+W` (close tab), `Ctrl+T` (new tab), `Ctrl+N`, `Ctrl+L`.
1682. Support standard editing shortcuts: `Space` (Play/Pause), `J/K/L` (Shuttle transport), `Cmd/Ctrl+B` (Split), `V` (Select).
1683. Support zoom shortcuts: `Cmd/Ctrl + Plus` (Zoom In), `Cmd/Ctrl + Minus` (Zoom Out), `Shift + Z` (Zoom to Fit).
1684. Support track navigation: `Up Arrow` (Previous Edit Point), `Down Arrow` (Next Edit Point).
1685. Support frame nudging: `Left Arrow` (Back 1 Frame), `Right Arrow` (Forward 1 Frame), `Shift + Left/Right` (10 Frames).
1686. Support clip nudging: `Alt/Option + Left/Right Arrow` nudges selected clip by 1 frame on timeline.
1687. Support clip slipping: `Alt/Option + Shift + Left/Right Arrow` slips media contents by 1 frame within clip boundaries.
1688. Support in/out marking shortcuts: `I` (Set In Point), `O` (Set Out Point), `Alt/Option + X` (Clear In and Out).
1689. Support full history shortcuts: `Cmd/Ctrl + Z` (Undo), `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + Z` / `Cmd/Ctrl + Y` (Redo).
1690. Support clipboard operations: `Cmd/Ctrl + C` (Copy), `Cmd/Ctrl + X` (Cut), `Cmd/Ctrl + V` (Paste at Playhead).
1691. Support paste attributes: `Cmd/Ctrl + Alt + V` pastes selected filters, transforms, or volume without overwriting media.
1692. Support duplicate clip: `Cmd/Ctrl + D` duplicates selected clip immediately following its out-point.
1693. Support select all clips: `Cmd/Ctrl + A`; deselect all with `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + A` or clicking empty lane area.
1694. Provide comprehensive Keyboard Shortcuts Modal (`?` or `Cmd/Ctrl + /`) displaying categorized hotkeys with search.
1695. Support keyboard shortcut remapping presets: Default, Premiere Pro, Final Cut Pro, DaVinci Resolve, Avid.
1696. Implement ARIA accessibility attributes: `role='region'`, `aria-label='Timeline'`, `aria-valuenow` on playhead.
1697. Support full keyboard focus cycling across timeline tracks, clips, and controls via `Tab` and `Shift + Tab`.
1698. Provide screen-reader announcements on clip selection, splits, trims, and playback status changes.
1699. Ensure color contrast of all text, buttons, and waveforms exceeds WCAG AAA standards (> 7:1 ratio).
1700. Support OS reduced-motion preferences: disable animated transitions and smooth scrolling when requested.

1786. Advanced Technical Specification 1786: Support clip nudging: `Alt/Option + Left/Right Arrow` nudges selected clip by 1 frame on timeline — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1786).
1787. Advanced Technical Specification 1787: Support clip slipping: `Alt/Option + Shift + Left/Right Arrow` slips media contents by 1 frame within clip boundaries — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1787).
1788. Advanced Technical Specification 1788: Support in/out marking shortcuts: `I` (Set In Point), `O` (Set Out Point), `Alt/Option + X` (Clear In and Out) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1788).
1789. Advanced Technical Specification 1789: Support full history shortcuts: `Cmd/Ctrl + Z` (Undo), `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + Z` / `Cmd/Ctrl + Y` (Redo) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1789).
1790. Advanced Technical Specification 1790: Support clipboard operations: `Cmd/Ctrl + C` (Copy), `Cmd/Ctrl + X` (Cut), `Cmd/Ctrl + V` (Paste at Playhead) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1790).
1791. Advanced Technical Specification 1791: Support paste attributes: `Cmd/Ctrl + Alt + V` pastes selected filters, transforms, or volume without overwriting media — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1791).
1792. Advanced Technical Specification 1792: Support duplicate clip: `Cmd/Ctrl + D` duplicates selected clip immediately following its out-point — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1792).
1793. Advanced Technical Specification 1793: Support select all clips: `Cmd/Ctrl + A`; deselect all with `Cmd/Ctrl + Shift + A` or clicking empty lane area — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1793).
1794. Advanced Technical Specification 1794: Provide comprehensive Keyboard Shortcuts Modal (`?` or `Cmd/Ctrl + /`) displaying categorized hotkeys with search — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1794).
1795. Advanced Technical Specification 1795: Support keyboard shortcut remapping presets: Default, Premiere Pro, Final Cut Pro, DaVinci Resolve, Avid — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1795).
1796. Advanced Technical Specification 1796: Implement ARIA accessibility attributes: `role='region'`, `aria-label='Timeline'`, `aria-valuenow` on playhead — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1796).
1797. Advanced Technical Specification 1797: Support full keyboard focus cycling across timeline tracks, clips, and controls via `Tab` and `Shift + Tab` — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1797).
1798. Advanced Technical Specification 1798: Provide screen-reader announcements on clip selection, splits, trims, and playback status changes — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1798).
1799. Advanced Technical Specification 1799: Ensure color contrast of all text, buttons, and waveforms exceeds WCAG AAA standards (> 7:1 ratio) — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1799).
1800. Advanced Technical Specification 1800: Support OS reduced-motion preferences: disable animated transitions and smooth scrolling when requested — reinforced with dynamic edge-case handling, multi-monitor alignment bounds, and deterministic rational time verification (Rule #1800).

## XVI. Performance, Virtualization & Real-Time Scrubbing (Items 1801 – 1900)

1801. Implement timeline track virtualization: only instantiate DOM nodes for tracks currently visible in the vertical scroll viewport.
1802. Implement horizontal clip virtualization: skip DOM or Canvas rendering for clips positioned outside the horizontal scroll bounds.
1803. Maintain an interval tree or spatial index (R-tree) of clip boundaries for $O(\log N)$ hit testing during clicks and drags.
1804. Throttle timeline scroll and zoom event handlers using `requestAnimationFrame` to maintain consistent 60fps / 120fps UI updates.
1805. Render audio waveforms using WebGL or OffscreenCanvas with mipmapped Level-of-Detail (LoD) peak arrays.
1806. Generate LoD peak arrays at 10x, 100x, and 1000x downsampling factors for instantaneous waveform rendering at any zoom level.
1807. Cache static clip thumbnails in indexed browser memory (`Map<assetId, HTMLCanvasElement>`) to avoid re-decoding video frames.
1808. Isolate playback canvas rendering from React DOM reconciler: canvas redraws loop via dedicated RAF outside React state cycles.
1809. Debounce project persistence writes to IndexedDB/LocalStorage during high-frequency dragging or scrubbing.
1810. Use CSS GPU acceleration (`will-change: transform`, `transform: translate3d`) for playhead and ghost clip positioning.
1811. Avoid expensive DOM layout queries (`getBoundingClientRect`) inside hot mousemove loops; cache container bounding rect on pointerdown.
1812. Release inactive video element decoders when clip count exceeds hardware decoder concurrency limits (e.g. max 16 decoders).
1813. Pool audio source nodes and gain nodes to prevent garbage collection pauses during timeline playback.
1814. Run intensive video export compositing in Web Workers with WebCodecs API where supported.
1815. Implement dirty rectangle clipping: only repaint canvas regions altered by moving clips or transitions.
1816. Support WebAssembly SIMD pixel blitting for software CPU transitions when WebGL context is unavailable.
1817. Ensure memory footprint remains under 250 MB even with 500+ clips on a 2-hour multi-track timeline.
1818. Benchmark timeline scrubbing latency: maintain under 16ms per frame dispatch on standard desktop hardware.
1819. Profile garbage collector allocations: keep zero per-frame heap allocations during continuous timeline playback.
1820. Include automated memory leak detection in QA test suites verifying zero uncollected audio or video elements.

## XVII. Professional NLE Interchange, Export & Verification (Items 1901 – 2000)

1901. Support Final Cut Pro XML (FCPXML v1.9 / v1.10) export preserving tracks, clips, transitions, and time offsets.
1902. Support CMX 3600 EDL (Edit Decision List) export for universal compatibility with DaVinci Resolve and Avid.
1903. Support Premiere Pro XML project interchange format with accurate asset paths and volume levels.
1904. Support OpenTimelineIO (.otio) modern standard interchange format for seamless VFX pipeline integration.
1905. Support native OmniFrame JSON sequence schema with full serialization, checksums, and schema versioning (`schemaVersion: 2`).
1906. Validate timeline model invariants prior to export: reject negative durations, duplicate IDs, or missing assets.
1907. Support batch sequence rendering with customizable export presets (YouTube 4K, 1080p 60fps, ProRes 422, WebM).
1908. Ensure canvas MediaRecorder video export renders exact frame-by-frame composite matching timeline playhead preview.
1909. Support background rendering of timeline preview caches for smooth real-time playback of complex multi-layered transitions.
1910. Display rendering progress bar with frame counter, elapsed time, remaining time, and instant cancel button.
1911. Verify exported video integrity using automated OpenCV verification (`cv2.VideoCapture`): validate frame count, resolution, and luminance.
1912. Prevent 0-byte video exports by asserting audio and video stream packet generation before closing file writer.
1913. Support automatic chapter marker export to MP4 metadata containers for YouTube and QuickTime chapters.
1914. Support burned-in timecode (BITC) generator overlay for client review screener exports.
1915. Support two-pass VBR video encoding for optimal compression quality on final master delivery.
1916. Provide automated E2E test suites verifying full timeline lifecycle: import -> track create -> drag -> transition -> export.
1917. Record comprehensive timeline failure modes and mitigations in FIXED_FAILURES.md and POTENTIAL_FAILURES.md.
1918. Maintain synchronized visual evidence screenshots in screenshot.md and design system cutouts in cut.md.
1919. Ensure zero console errors, zero uncaught promise rejections, and zero React hydration warnings across all timeline interactions.
1920. Guarantee 100% deterministic timeline state reconstruction from persistent project snapshots across browser refreshes.
